An anchor-based fidelity term keeps the optimized noises near plausible regions, and Lagrangian relaxation gives a two-term objective with a single hyperparameter $\lambda$ governing the diversity-quality trade-off, without training or sampler modification. Across three backbones (SD1.5, SDXL, SD3), DetNO achieves leading intra-batch diversity in VendiScore and Mean Self-Similarity, and a downstream few-shot classification experiment confirms that broader mode coverage brings statistically significant accuracy gains for synthetic data augmentation in the low-shot regime. Fixing the pipeline and varying only the batch objective, both RBF-based aggregators outperform InfoNCE, and the log-determinant attains the best downstream accuracy on both benchmarks. Its diversity advantage over InfoNCE widens as the batch grows. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SCOPE: Semantic Bounded Credit Optimization for Prompt Engineering in Text-to-image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22264)**  `arXiv:2610.22264`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yufei Jiao, Jinfa Huang, Zeyue Xue, Tianyu Xie, Yuexiao Ma, Rongrong Ji, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reinforcement learning (RL) enables language models to improve text-to-image (T2I) generation by rewriting prompts for a frozen image generator. Existing methods typically apply a single signal derived from the image to individual subword tokens, although objects, counts, attributes, and relations form coherent semantic spans. We propose SCOPE, an objective based on Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) that aligns update weighting with this structure. The rewriter jointly generates an enhanced prompt and its semantic partition. SCOPE pools shifts in policy log probabilities within each span to derive a shared coefficient for its token losses while retaining a single advantage for each completion. It requires neither separate span rewards nor additional image renders. Across Qwen-Image, SD3, and FLUX.1-dev, SCOPE achieves average relative gains over vanilla GRPO of 9.48% on GeneVal and 7.62% on T2I-CompBench. Ablation studies show that both semantic boundaries and pooling within spans contribute to these gains, supporting semantic structure as a practical inductive bias for prompt engineering with RL.
  </details>

- **[FPG-Seg: Annotation-free Retinal Vessel Topology Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22259)**  `arXiv:2610.22259`  `cs.CV`  
  _James Willoughby, Irina Voiculescu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  State-of-the art approaches to retinal vessel segmentation typically require extensive annotations, a highly time-consuming process, reliant on clinical expertise. We present FPG-Seg, an annotation-free pipeline for retinal vessel segmentation combining classical vesselness filtering, Bayesian deep learning and topology-aware graph reconstruction to segment retinal vessel topology without manual labels. Tested across four widely used benchmark datasets for retinal vessel segmentation, FPG-Seg attains a clDice of $0.74{-}0.78$, representing $86{-}98\%$ of the performance of an equivalent fully-supervised baseline, despite using no manual annotations for training or hyperparameter tuning. As part of this work, we also provide, to our knowledge, the first performance benchmark of an annotation-free vessel segmentation method on FIVES.
  </details>

- **[WaveLLM: Wavelet-Domain LLM Entropy Modeling for Lossless Image Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22232)**  `arXiv:2610.22232`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yue Yu, Bowei Kang, Changsheng Gao, Dong Liu, Xiaoyan Sun_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Lossless image compression fundamentally relies on accurate entropy modeling, driving a recent shift toward large language model (LLM)-based methods. However, existing approaches mainly emphasize the modeling capacity of LLMs while directly serializing images into pixel or residual sequences, leaving heterogeneous signal patterns entangled within a single prediction stream. In this paper, we argue that effective entropy modeling depends not only on model capacity, but also on the predictability of the input representation. Motivated by this insight, we propose WaveLLM, a wavelet-domain LLM-based entropy model for lossless image compression. By decomposing an image into multi-level wavelet subbands, WaveLLM converts a heterogeneous prediction problem into a set of more structured and statistically homogeneous subproblems that are more amenable to autoregressive probability estimation. To bridge numerical coding symbols with the LLM's native text token space, we introduce a dedicated numerical interface featuring value embeddings and a coefficient prediction head. Furthermore, a task-adaptive prompting strategy is designed to enable a single shared model to adapt to diverse subbands within a unified framework. Built on Llama-3.2-3B with LoRA adaptation, WaveLLM achieves 2.51, 2.24, and 2.27 bpsp on Kodak,this http URL, and DIV2K, respectively, outperforming the PixelCNN-based codec using the same wavelet transform by 0.43, 0.36, and 0.46 bpsp, and surpassing recent LLM-based lossless compressors on all three benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[OpenGCUA: Toward a Fully Open Stack for Generalist Computer Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22200)**  `arXiv:2610.22200`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hanzhang Zhou, Long Li, Quyu Kong, Lei Wang, Panrong Tong, Long Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Computer use has reached a turning point: the focus is expanding from GUI-only interaction to systems that complete tasks on users' computers through visual and programmatic interfaces. We define a generalist computer-use agent (GCUA) as a system in which the model chooses how to observe, act, and organize its work across visual and programmatic interfaces. Yet frontier implementations, such as GPT-6 Astra's computer-use system, remain largely closed, limiting systematic study of their design. We introduce Open Generalist CUA (OpenGCUA), an ongoing project to develop and open the full stack for GCUAs with the community. This report presents the first release of OpenGCUA. Code execution is a key feature of our agent design: it lets intermediate results determine subsequent operations and enables models to apply their existing programming capabilities directly to computer interaction. We also propose harness-to-model decision transfer, moving selected observations and task-state decisions from fixed harness rules to the model. On OSWorld-v2, our full configuration improves task scores across all eight evaluated models, including GPT-6 Astra and Claude Opus 5, raising mean partial score by +19.6 points and reducing reported steps per task by an average of 53.0% relative to batched GUI-only execution. We release our implementation and prompts, evaluation trajectories from all eight models, and preliminary versions of a real-device harness and a cross-device SDK. The harness addresses real-device challenges not fully captured by benchmarks, while the SDK provides a common programming interface across computers, mobiles and browsers. Across four real macOS tasks under our real-device harness, selected Qwen3.8-Max runs use 19.4 times as many reported reasoning tokens as GPT-6 Astra, highlighting substantial differences between models in real-device execution efficiency.
  </details>

- **[SCCL-DT: Semantic-Conflict-Aware Contrastive Learning with Dual-Rule Teacher Distillation for Zero-Shot Chest X-Ray Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22094)**  `arXiv:2610.22094`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ting Liu, Haoran Lai, HaobinChen, Ming Liu, S Kevin Zhou_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Contrastive vision-language pre-training supports zero-shot chest X-ray diagnosis, but standard objectives treat every off-diagonal image-report pair in a batch as a negative. In radiology, recurring findings across patients mean that instance mismatch need not imply semantic incompatibility, creating false-negative supervision that we term a semantic conflict. A training-trace audit shows that pair-observations selected by the online mask used during training are enriched for report-derived finding agreement, although this proxy analysis does not establish clinical equivalence. Fixed thresholds can become miscalibrated as similarity distributions evolve, while soft reweighting leaves residual repulsive gradients. We therefore propose Semantic-Conflict-Aware Contrastive Learning (SCCL), which tracks modality-specific similarity distributions with exponential-moving-average (EMA) statistics, derives adaptive thresholds, and hard-masks flagged candidates before softmax normalization. Inclusive (OR) and consensus (AND) rules define two SCCL teachers, whose bidirectional cross-modal rankings are consolidated through standard distillation into a single deployable student, SCCL-DT. Across five public chest X-ray benchmarks, SCCL-DT achieves a mean zero-shot AUC of $0.850$, compared with $0.837$ for the strongest evaluated baseline in our comparison. Code will be made publicly available soon.
  </details>

- **[Retrofitting Recurrent Vision-Language Models from Pretrained Backbones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22075)**  `arXiv:2610.22075`  `cs.CV`  
  _Madhav Iyengar, Andreas Geiger, Hilde Kühne_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent work shows that depth recurrence can be retrofitted into pretrained language models. In this work, we adapt this paradigm for visual understanding, using depth recurrence to reduce the parameter footprint of a pretrained vision-language model (VLM). To this end, we prune layers from the language backbone of the VLM and loop over a subset of the remaining layers. At each step of the loop, a linear adapter merges the block's output with the token representations from the preceding layers and projects the fused representation back into the block. As the pruning impacts the original model performance, we propose a three-stage recovery training: first, we leverage a language-only pretraining to recover the performance of the language backbone, followed by an alignment of the vision-language projector, and a full fine-tuning of the model via visual instruction tuning. In the first and the last stage, we distill the outputs of the pretrained non-recurrent model into the recurrent model, which further improves the recovery. We evaluate the proposed approach on the TinyLLaVA-1.4B backbone without introducing any new vision-language data during retrofitting. Our experiments demonstrate that the retrofitted recurrent model outperforms the unpruned baseline trained with the same recovery data while storing 23.3% fewer language parameters. We further show that the proposed retrofitting recurrence outperforms the setup of converting a recurrent LM into a VLM on the same data. Additionally, our ablations show how design choices, such as the adapter between recurrences and a larger recurrent block, lead to the improved performance of our approach. Finally, we find that our proposed model gains on reasoning-oriented benchmarks, whereas its performance on basic visual understanding benchmarks remains on par with the baseline.
  </details>

- **[FeaTok: Feature-Loss Tokenization for Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22060)**  `arXiv:2610.22060`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhang Xiaofeng, Samuel Lavoie, Nicolas Beltran-Velez, Jianhao Yuan, Felix Friedrich, Jakob Verbeek, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Discrete image generators learn the distribution of tokens produced by an image tokenizer. These token representations should be easy for generators to model, not merely enable faithful image reconstruction. Yet these tokenizers are commonly trained to reconstruct pixels, which prioritizes low-level fidelity and may disrupt representation structure useful for generation. Motivated by feature-predictive self-supervised learning, we introduce feature-loss tokenization (FeaTok), which trains discrete tokens by reconstructing pretrained features rather than pixels. Ablations across SigLIP, SigLIP2, DINOv2, and DINOv3 show better generative performance with FeaTok. In class-conditional generation, FeaTok advances the quality-compute Pareto frontier, reducing gFDr6 at 256x256 by up to 29 percent over the strongest discrete baselines and outperforming the strongest unguided continuous baseline (RAEv2) with 38 times less sampling compute. FeaTok also achieves higher human-preference reward-model scores than prior art without reward-based training and surpasses continuous and discrete text-conditional baselines on three benchmarks with only 500 fine-tuning updates after pre-training.
  </details>

- **[When Models Misread Ink: Auditing VLMs for Arabic Calligraphy Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22018)**  `arXiv:2610.22018`  `cs.CV`  
  _Niaz Mohaiman Abtahi, Evan Maksud, Reyana Shahid_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Arabic calligraphy is a high-stakes cultural visual domain for Muslim communities, yet VLM evaluations rarely test its fine-grained script traditions. We audit 12 VLMs on a cleaned 1,195-image six-way benchmark using numeric, visual-cue, and Diwani-first prompts. In this closed-set setting, the best numeric-prompt accuracy is 60.9% for closed/API models and 47.2% for open-weight models. The main failure is default-label collapse: every numeric run is dominated by a single label taking 33-63% of predictions, against an 18.8% majority-class baseline. For 8 of 12 models the default is Thuluth; GPT-4o labels 52.9% of all images as Thuluth although it is only 18.1% of the set. Prompting helps unevenly: visual cues move accuracy by more than 10 points for some models but hurt others, and foregrounding Diwani often relocates the default rather than removing it. Arabic calligraphy recognition is therefore a compact, culturally grounded stress test for VLM robustness and for measuring which visual traditions become legible to automated systems.
  </details>

- **[Visual Sanad: Auditing Vision-Language Models for False Acceptance of Qur'anic Citations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22009)**  `arXiv:2610.22009`  `cs.CV`  
  _Niaz Mohaiman Abtahi, Reyana Shahid, Evan Maksud_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly used to interpret religious, educational, and culturally sensitive visual content, yet their reliability on citation verification remains underexplored. We introduce Visual Sanad, a controlled benchmark for testing whether VLMs falsely accept incorrect Qur'anic verse attributions when the same quote-reference pair is rendered under different visual framings. The benchmark contains 2,400 rendered images built from 100 English Qur'an verses, four factual states per verse, and six visual conditions ranging from plain text to decorative and authority-framed layouts. All quote-reference pairs were source-checked and human-audited before rendering. Across 24 full model-prompt runs, we observe a sharp capability split. Proprietary API-served VLMs range from near-perfect citation verification to moderate false acceptance, whereas recent open-weight VLMs often show weak discrimination and default to VALID on most images. De-biased prompting can reduce false acceptance, but often by shifting the decision threshold toward INVALID rather than by producing strong citation discrimination. Visual Sanad therefore exposes a culturally grounded multimodal factuality failure: fluent VLMs may treat displayed religious citations as self-authenticating unless they have strong citation-verification ability.
  </details>

- **[Data-Efficient ConvNeXt Pretraining for High-Resolution Sentinel-1 GRD SAR Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21993)**  `arXiv:2610.21993`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuki Natsume, Sang-Ho Yun, Chai Kiat Yeo_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Labeled Sentinel-1 Ground Range Detected (S1GRD) datasets remain scarce, limiting supervised deep learning for land-surface remote sensing, while self-supervised pretraining for this domain has been dominated by Vision Transformer (ViT)-based masked image modeling requiring substantial data and compute. We propose SparK-SAR, a sparse convolutional masked pretraining framework built on SparK and ConvNeXt, designed to exploit convolutional inductive biases for S1GRD imagery under constrained compute. SparK-SAR makes three core technical contributions: (i) ConvNeXt-RS, a domain-adapted encoder that halves the stem stride (4x to 2x) to produce 14x14 feature maps at stride 16x, providing 4x more spatial tokens at identical parameter count; (ii) a lightweight ConvNeXt-512 reconstruction decoder with PixelShuffle upsampling that reduces pretraining VRAM by 7.5-8.5% per GPU relative to a UNet-128 baseline while matching its accuracy; and (iii) an empirical characterization of FP16 mixed-precision pretraining instability in the standard SparK UNet-768 decoder on S1GRD data, complemented by a finding that finetuning with layer-wise learning rate decay (LLRD) improves downstream performance of pretrained encoders. On four Copernicus-Bench S1GRD tasks, our finetuned 27.8M-parameter ConvNeXt-RS encoder improves over a non-pretrained baseline by +4.7, +5.0, +5.8, and +1.4 pp on EuroSAT-S1, BigEarthNet-S1, DFC2020-S1, and Flood-S1 respectively, and outperforms the Copernicus-FM ViT (139M parameters, frozen) on three of four tasks at roughly 5x fewer parameters. A pretraining data-scaling study reveals task-dependent data efficiency: classification saturates with as little as 10-25% of the SSL4EO-S12 v1.1 corpus, while dense prediction continues to improve significantly up to 25-50%. The study also includes a CKA representation analysis and a geographic de-collision experiment.
  </details>

- **[Flow-CLIP: Image-Conditioned Residual Scoring for CompositionalImage-Text Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21937)**  `arXiv:2610.21937`  `cs.CV`  
  _Minghong Wei, Enguang Zhu, Pu Cao, Lu Yang, Qing Song_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision--language pre-training has enabled substantial progress in image--text retrieval and zero-shot recognition by learning transferable cross-modal representations. However, sentence-level alignment remains insufficient for compositional matching, where captions can describe the same objects and attributes while differing in how these elements are bound. To address this limitation, we propose Flow-CLIP, which refines global image-text alignment through image-conditioned changes in token--patch evidence. Global Compatibility Calibration (GCC) learns a sentence-level similarity metric, while Image-Conditioned Evidence Refinement (ICER) incorporates visual patch context into token queries and uses the resulting change in local matching evidence to adjust the global score. We further introduce Cross-Image Margin Ranking (CIMR), which compares the same positive--negative caption pair across matched and mismatched images, encouraging a larger ranking margin when the image supports the correct description. Experiments on compositional matching and attribute--object binding benchmarks show that Flow-CLIP improves binding accuracy over pretrained CLIP and learned global alignment through local evidence refinement, while cross-image ranking supervision mitigates reliance on language priors. Code will be released at link.
  </details>

- **[Seeing is Believing: Benchmarking and Mitigating the Interference of Language Priors on Visual Perception in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21902)**  `arXiv:2610.21902`  `cs.CV`  
  _Li Ma, Yan Zhuang, Jiawen Deng, Fuji Ren_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Language priors in MLLMs can override visual evidence, impairing visual perception. Existing benchmarks assume shared priors across models and rely solely on accuracy. However, cross-model prior agreement falls below 80%, and over 15% of items can be answered correctly without any image, causing accuracy to overestimate prior resistance. To address these limitations, we introduce ViSiP-Bench, a benchmark grounded in rare-but-real scenarios across six prior categories, with a model-aware dynamic prior induction protocol and three metrics that more precisely and continuously measure prior resistance than accuracy alone. Evaluating 24 models reveals that prior adherence is pervasive, does not improve with scale, and larger models tend to either strongly override their priors or firmly adhere to them. We therefore propose MAPS, which identifies each model's strongest priors and fine-tunes on factually grounded knowledge to soften excessive prior reliance without disrupting general capabilities.
  </details>

- **[From Adults to Children: Benchmarking Foundation Models for Chest X-Ray Classification under Domain Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21809)**  `arXiv:2610.21809`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sara Inácio, Hugo Proença, João C. Neves_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Foundation models have shown significant potential across diverse fields and have been applied to medical imaging, but their robustness across patient populations remains unclear. In this work, we investigate foundation models' ability to generalize from adult to pediatric chest X-ray classification. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first benchmark to compare multiple foundation models under a common adult-to-pediatric evaluation setting while also analyzing the effect of adaptation depth. We harmonize VinDr-CXR and PediCXR into a five-label classification task and evaluate six foundation models and two CNN baselines. All models are adapted using adult data only and tested in both the in-domain and pediatric domains using linear probing, partial fine-tuning, and full fine-tuning. The benchmark reveals a clear and consistent trend: all models suffer a substantial performance degradation when transferred from adult to pediatric data, with drops exceeding 10 percentage points. While Macro-AUROC values reach approximately 92% on the adult test set, performance decreases to around 78% on the pediatric domain. Another important finding is that increasing the depth of adaptation does not significantly improve cross-population generalization. Moreover, strong in-domain performance does not translate into better performance on pediatric data.
  </details>

- **[Teaching DINOv3 About Partial 3D Geometry: A Self-Supervised Geometry-Aware Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21802)**  `arXiv:2610.21802`  `cs.CV`  
  _Viktoria Ehm, Dongliang Cao, Riccardo Marin, Daniel Scholz, Weikang Wang, Florian Bernard, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Partial shape matching is a crucial yet underexplored problem in 3D vision, with significant relevance to real-world scenarios where shapes are often only partially observed. Existing feature descriptors face difficulties in this setting, as traditional representations either struggle with the boundaries of partial shapes or heavily depend on the shape's spatial position. While existing approaches have employed DINO features for partial shape matching, these features are not inherently suited for handling partial observations. In this work, we propose a method to refine DINO features using LoRA-based self-supervised learning, enabling the generation of feature descriptors that are robust to partiality. Our features substantially improve performance on partial shape matching compared to traditional or vision foundation features. Additionally, when integrated into existing partial shape matching pipelines, we achieve state-of-the-art results on partial shape matching and left-right prediction benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[Learning to Think and Explore in 3D World](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21783)**  `arXiv:2610.21783`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yijiang Li, Bingyang Wang, Nuno Vasconcelos_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We study how vision-language models (VLMs) can learn to think and actively explore in 3D space. Instead of providing multi-view images, videos, or 3D representations to the model, we preserve the native visual encoding of VLMs, i.e., a single image, one at a time, just like humans, and treat the 3D representation only as an environment with which the model can interact through actions (e.g., move left) and observations (rendered images). Each rollout begins from an initial first-person view and a visual question. The model can then iteratively reason, navigate, and reorient its viewpoint before committing to a final answer. Following an initial trajectory-supervised finetuning, we apply reinforcement learning (RL) with rewards driven primarily by final-answer correctness. Crucially, RL provides no direct supervision over which viewpoints the model should visit or which exploration actions it should take. Instead, the model must learn an effective exploration policy solely from the extent to which its interactions improve performance. Across six 3D benchmarks, our model ACT3D achieves an average accuracy of 53.2% that improves its base Qwen model by 22.0% and surpasses proprietary Gemini 3.6 Flash. ACT3D also outperforms existing open-source 3D methods while using 50-70% fewer input frames, and generalizes to unseen domains with an average improvement of 5.9% over its supervised initialization. Further analysis reveals adaptive, question-conditioned exploration. Performance gains over the base model increase from 0.6% on questions answerable from the initial view to 16.3% on those requiring extensive exploration. ACT3D searches for occluded targets, avoids unnecessary exploration, and acquires complementary viewpoints for spatial comparisons. These findings demonstrate that outcome-based RL enables VLMs not only to reason over visual evidence, but also to learn when and how to acquire it.
  </details>

- **[FuseAdapt: Parameter-Efficient Multimodal Fusion for Semantic Segmentation with Missing Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21779)**  `arXiv:2610.21779`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xin Zhang, Robby T. Tan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-modal semantic segmentation benefits from complementary sensors, but this benefit is fragile when some modalities are degraded or missing at test time. Many existing methods rely on dedicated fusion modules or multi-branch cross-modal encoders, and the fusion often becomes unreliable when modalities are absent, leading to substantial performance drops. We propose \textbf{FuseAdapt}, a parameter-efficient framework that performs fusion within the adaptation space of a frozen vision foundation model (VFM), exploring whether VFM priors can serve as an effective shared backbone for multi-modal segmentation. Instead of attaching a standalone fusion network, FuseAdapt assigns each modality a lightweight adaptation branch and performs \emph{adaptation-space fusion} by keeping Q/V low-rank adaptation private to each modality and routing only FFN low-rank residual activations across modalities inside the frozen backbone. The pretrained VFM remains fixed while modality-specific corrections and explicit cross-modal interaction are learned through the adaptation paths. To handle missing modalities, we further introduce \textbf{Predictive Modality Modeling (PMM)}, a direct representation-level objective for cross-modal redundancy. During training, PMM masks a target modality and predicts its embedding from the visible ones, supervised by a teacher target computed under full-modality input. PMM is used only during training and adds no inference-time overhead. Across three multi-modal segmentation benchmarks (MCubeS, DELIVER, MUSES), FuseAdapt consistently improves both modality-complete and modality-incomplete segmentation with a small trainable budget, yielding up to +3.01 mIoU under full modalities and +12.56 mIoU under missing modalities over prior state-of-the-art. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Bridging the Markerless Tracking Validation Gap: Intraoperative X-ray Workflow Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21696)**  `arXiv:2610.21696`  `cs.CV`  
  _Connor Daly, Salvatore Russo, Jinendra Ekanayake, Daniel S. Elson, Ferdinando Rodriguez y Baena_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Markerless intraoperative tracking of spinal anatomy via RGB-D sensors is a promising alternative to invasive bone-anchored fiducials, yet no existing clinical dataset provides independent subsurface ground truth for validating these methods. We address this gap with a clinically viable pipeline that derives per-vertebra 6-DoF pose from routine intraoperative C-arm fluoroscopy, co-registered to the RGB-D camera frame through a multimodal fiducial array detectable in both imaging modalities. Critically, the pipeline requires no additional radiation, instrumentation, or deviation from standard surgical workflows, relying solely on the fluoroscopic images already acquired during routine level checks. A calibrated transform chain links the vertebral pose recovered via multi-view 2D-3D CT-to-fluoroscopy registration to the RGB-D coordinate frame, enabling direct comparison of any markerless registration estimate against a subsurface reference. Ex vivo validation against optically tracked ovine vertebrae confirms end-to-end accuracy of 2.06 +- 0.29 mm and 3.35 +- 0.93 degrees, sufficient to serve as independent ground truth for methods with substantially larger errors. We demonstrate the pipeline's utility by evaluating two markerless registration strategies on clinical data spanning 11 vertebrae across 1,500 frames from three surgical sequences: the first such evaluation against an independent subsurface reference in a clinical setting. The proposed framework provides reusable infrastructure for benchmarking emerging markerless tracking methods on real surgical data.
  </details>

- **[FusionAudit: Pathway-Conditioned Robustness Auditing for Native Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21667)**  `arXiv:2610.21667`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiaming Zhang, Zherui Li, Siqi Guo, Junhao Dong, Kun Wang, Wei Yang Bryan Lim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As vision-language models (VLMs) transition from frozen-encoder pipelines to native multimodal architectures, the visual channel increasingly serves as both a perceptual input and a carrier of rendered text that competes with user prompts. Current single-attack, pixel-centric evaluations fail to capture this complexity: they collapse distinct visual attack surfaces into a single scalar and ignore how architectural shifts alter vulnerability. We introduce PaRMA, an evidence-based auditing framework that defines pathway-conditioned risk over two primary image-level interventions: pixel perturbation and rendered-text insertion. Instead of arbitrary attack enumeration, PaRMA uses pathway-specific diagnostics to expose architecture-specific blind spots. Notably, we uncover a see-but-not-deceived signature where native VLMs attend heavily to typographic overlays but visually reject them, and we identify an objective mismatch between encoder-level and answer-level attacks in deep-fusion models. To exploit these findings, we instantiate FusionAudit, introducing two diagnostic-motivated attacks: MANTIS (margin-aware pixel perturbation) and Nat-Evid (host-congruent semantic insertion). Evaluating 18 open-weight VLMs across a novel nativeness taxonomy, we demonstrate that legacy single-attack rankings (e.g., PGD) are unstable for native models. Our diagnostic-driven attacks substantially raise the estimated residual risk, showing that PaRMA provides a falsifiable, evidence-driven auditing methodology for uncovering vulnerabilities missed by standard benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[Digital Typhoon Dataset V3: Benchmarking for Multi-spectral Extension and Analyzing Systematic Bias in Intensity Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21654)**  `arXiv:2610.21654`  `cs.CV`  
  _Asanobu Kitamoto, Victor Le Louarn, Noelia Falczuk, Ngoc Anh Tong, Anja Veleva_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This paper presents the Digital Typhoon Dataset V3, a new version of the longest typhoon satellite image dataset spanning nearly 50 years, designed to benchmark machine learning models on long-term spatio-temporal data. The latest addition to Dataset V3 is multispectral satellite data, enabling multi-channel models. We investigate data-splitting strategies and present benchmarking results for the Northern and Southern Hemispheres. Experimental results show that, under the current dataset construction and experimental setting, multi-channel inputs do not clearly outperform single-channel inputs for pressure regression. Among the evaluated model architectures, image types, and spectral channels, ResNet-18, full-resolution images, and Channel 1 generally provide the best single-channel performance for pressure regression. Performance also differs between the WP and AU datasets by regression target, with lower RMSE on WP for pressure regression and on AU for wind regression. These results highlight the importance of considering dataset-specific and task-specific characteristics when interpreting benchmark performance. Additionally, we analyzed systematic bias in intensity regression, which leads to underestimation of intensity for strong typhoons. We tested imbalance-aware weighting strategies, including inverse-density weighting inspired by BalancedMSE, and showed that these approaches are not sufficient to remove the bias. The dataset is publicly available atthis http URLandthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SCMerge: Spatial-Consistency-Aware Token Pair Merging for Efficient Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21642)**  `arXiv:2610.21642`  `cs.CV`  
  _Junfeng Ren, Yi Gong, Yuan Zeng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision Transformer architectures have showcased impressive performance on semantic segmentation, yet their quadratic attention complexity hinders practical applications. Recent works have focused on merging tokens to reduce sequence length and thus improve transformer efficiency. However, existing token merging methods primarily rely on feature similarity to identify similar tokens, overlooking spatial consistency, which may introduce segmentation errors in semantic segmentation. In this work, we propose SCMerge, a training-free token merging method that jointly considers feature similarity and spatial consistency in pairing similar tokens. The pairwise tokens are then merged within the encoder, and the resulting token sequences are reconstructed onto the original patch grid after decoding. The proposed method is flexible and can be plugged into existing vision Transformer-based semantic segmentation pipelines. Extensive experiments on the benchmark datasets ADE20K, Pascal Context, and Cityscapes show that SCMerge consistently achieves a better trade-off between segmentation accuracy and efficiency than baseline methods.
  </details>

- **[Anchor3DGS: Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting with Compact Anchor-Based Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21634)**  `arXiv:2610.21634`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sheng Ye, Jiangke Lin, Fudong Wang, Yi Yuan, Zhen-Hui Dong, Yong-Jin Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) methods have enabled effective multi-view 3D reconstruction by predicting Gaussian primitives in a single forward pass. However, existing approaches predominantly adopt a pixel-aligned paradigm that predicts one Gaussian per input pixel, coupling the Gaussian count to the input resolution and number of views. This also leads to redundant primitives in textureless regions and insufficient coverage in geometrically complex areas. We present Anchor3DGS, a pose-free feed-forward 3DGS framework that replaces pixel-aligned prediction with a compact anchor-based representation. Guided by information entropy, our method places a set of 3D anchors in the scene, concentrating them in regions of higher visual complexity while maintaining broad spatial coverage. Each anchor performs occlusion-aware aggregation of multi-view image features, exchanges information with other anchors, and is decoded into a set of local Gaussians. A lightweight feature enhancement module further refines the coarse RGB output from Gaussian splatting and improves rendering fidelity. Extensive experiments on diverse benchmark datasets demonstrate that our Anchor3DGS achieves state-of-the-art novel view synthesis quality while only using an order of magnitude fewer Gaussian primitives.
  </details>

- **[MYOPIA: Understanding the Limits of Tree-Based Reasoning in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21573)**  `arXiv:2610.21573`  `cs.CV`  
  _Islam Mesabah, Sary Elmansoury, Gerrit Großmann, Peter Neigel, Sebastian J. Vollmer_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Decision trees can make vision-language model (VLM) classification interpretable by replacing a single prediction with a sequence of root-to-leaf questions. However, the accuracy cost of this greedy, non-backtracking procedure remains unclear. We introduce \textbf{MYOPIA}, a benchmark comprising four LLM-generated, human-refined decision trees for GTSRB, MNIST, CIFAR-10, and MSCOCO, together with matched prompts, diagnostic protocols, and an evaluation harness. Across GPT-4o, Qwen-VL Max, and LLaMA-3.2 11B Vision, tree traversal underperforms zero-shot prompting in all 12 dataset--model pairs; paired bootstrap analyses consistently favor zero-shot. Our diagnostics characterize interacting failure modes associated with this gap. Models often recall tree-encoded class attributes more accurately than they ground the same attributes in images: the mean label--image accuracy gaps are 9.5, 13.8, and 12.6 points for GPT-4o, Qwen-VL Max, and LLaMA, respectively. Incorrect local decisions then irreversibly exclude the correct class, while answer extraction introduces additional model-dependent loss. A node-level audit of the four trees finds that split entropy is weakly but consistently associated with lower node accuracy, whereas depth, branching factor, and within-node grounding differences are less stable. \textbf{MYOPIA} therefore provides a framework for diagnosing hierarchical visual classification and motivates testing tree designs that prioritize perceptual answerability and recovery from early errors. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Decoupled Safety Control: A Safety-Control Algorithm for Training-Free Safety Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21569)**  `arXiv:2610.21569`  `cs.CV`  
  _Huilin Zhou, Ruoxi Cheng, Yuhang Wang, Yuming Liu, Minghao Sun, Ruolong Ma, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion models achieve strong performance in text-to-image generation, but they can still produce unsafe content. Training-free safety guidance offers a practical inference-time alternative that intervenes during sampling without modifying model weights. However, many existing safeguards still rely on a coupled safety-control chain. In this chain, the same unsafe condition simultaneously specifies what should be avoided, indicates the current unsafe signal, constructs the intervention, and determines when that intervention remains active. This coupling can work when unsafe factors are explicit, but becomes less reliable once harmful and benign semantics are entangled in the current generation state, often leaving residual unsafe content or unnecessarily degrading benign-generation quality or prompt fidelity. We address this problem with Decoupled Safety Control (DSC), a plug-in safety-control algorithm for training-free safety guidance. DSC reformulates training-free safety guidance as an explicit state-dependent control process, so the executed intervention is determined from the current generation state rather than inherited directly from a single unsafe condition. Experiments on standard safety benchmarks show that DSC improves representative latent- and text-space safeguards, achieving stronger unsafe-content suppression while preserving benign-generation quality and maintaining efficient inference.
  </details>

- **[RLDF: A Region-Level Deepfake Dataset and Benchmark for Partial-Face Manipulation Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21438)**  `arXiv:2610.21438`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shukesh Reddy, Abhijit Das_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing deepfake detection models are built upon manipu- lated images that are tampered with either exclusively fully swapped or fully synthesised faces, which leads us to an open question: Can off-the-shelf deepfake models detect manipulation effectively while tampering is confined to a local facial sub-region? To fill this gap, we introduce RLDF (Region-Level Deepfake), the first dataset for region-level deepfake evaluation. Employing the proposed region-aware partial swap synthesis model, we generate over 2M par- tial face-swap images by using eight face-swapping meth- ods. Altogether, we considered seven face regions, among which there are four discrete regions (eye, nose, mouth and eyebrow) and four composite facial regions (eye and nose; eye, nose and mouth; eye, nose, mouth and eye- brow; and full face). In addition, we define three evalu- ation protocols and benchmark 12 state-of-the-art detectors across the five popular standard cross-dataset evaluations, DF40, and the proposed RLDF, providing a comprehen- sive audit of where and why existing detectors underper- formed. Our evaluation reveals that existing detectors fail to generalize consistently across partial facial regions, re- sulting in an overall drop of 10.5% AUC and 10% accu- racy under region-level evaluation. Further, we propose a mask-guided cross-attention model as a baseline approach that can effectively detect region-level manipulation. The proposed dataset, code, and checkpoints are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Perception or Reasoning? Diagnosing and Tackling Reinforcement Learning Bottlenecks in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21380)**  `arXiv:2610.21380`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zeyuan Yang, Zhuohao Chen, Jiaben Chen, Yao Zhang, Maohao Shen, Chuang Gan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reinforcement learning has brought strong gains to language model reasoning, but its improvements on visual tasks remain less consistent. One difficulty is that visual task failures can arise from different sources: the same benchmark may be limited by perception for one model and by reasoning for another. We introduce Perception and Reasoning Attribution (PARA), which measures the remaining perceptual and reasoning headroom of each model--benchmark pair by comparing how many current errors are recovered with more accurate visual information versus a stronger reasoner. Across 4 vision-language models and 17 multimodal benchmarks, PARA broadly agrees with the expected characteristics of existing benchmarks while revealing substantial model-specific variation. More importantly, it shows that RL gains become smaller as perceptual headroom increases, but larger when reasoning explains more of the recoverable errors, suggesting that current RL exploits reasoning headroom more effectively than perceptual headroom. Motivated by this gap, we analyze how existing RL objectives affect visual perception and find that reward- and token-level signals influence visual attention only indirectly. We therefore propose GRAD, a group-relative attention distillation objective that uses the best rollout in each group to directly guide the visual attention of weaker rollouts. GRAD consistently improves performance across two model scales and seven benchmarks, and further complements existing RL methods.
  </details>

- **[SATOR: Backpropagation-Free 3D Test-Time Adaptation via Sample-Adaptive Token Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21367)**  `arXiv:2610.21367`  `cs.CV`  
  _Inseok Kong, Geunyoung Jung, Jiyoung Jung_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Test-time adaptation (TTA) mitigates the performance degradation of source-pretrained models on unlabeled test data under distribution shifts. Recent backpropagation-free token-purging methods address 3D point cloud distribution shifts by filtering unreliable tokens. However, their reliance on predefined purge-size candidates requires multiple forward passes per sample and increases inference cost. We propose SATOR (Sample-Adaptive TOken Routing), a source-free and backpropagation-free 3D TTA framework that eliminates this search by deriving both the partitioning threshold and routing decision directly from each sample. SATOR computes token abnormality scores from complementary attention and geometric relation graphs. It then derives a sample-specific partition into retained and pruned tokens via Otsu-based separability, without evaluating candidate sizes. Keep-ratio-calibrated routing then selects the pruned prediction only when its entropy, penalized by the keep ratio, is lower than that of the original prediction. Experiments on ModelNet40-C, ScanObjectNN-C, and ShapeNet-C demonstrate that SATOR achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on three 3D corruption benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[VLA-$Δ$: Learning Physical State Transition for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21306)**  `arXiv:2610.21306`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qi Lu, Jiarun Li, Ce Zhang, Miao Pan, Yi Pan, Xu Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language-action (VLA) models transfer semantic knowledge acquired through vision-language pretraining to robot control, achieving strong performance across various benchmarks. However, most VLAs directly map observations to demonstrated actions without explicit supervision of intermediate physical state changes, making them prone to overfitting observation-action correspondences. Some methods introduce intermediate reasoning or future-state prediction in language, visual, or latent spaces to guide action generation, but do not explicitly supervise the physical state transition itself. We therefore propose VLA-$\Delta$, a general framework that uses physical changes between current and future states as explicit learning targets. To support this objective, we construct a large-scale pretraining dataset and derive physical state-transition annotations covering task-relevant semantics and visual motion changes, providing more direct supervision for continuous action generation. During inference, the model generates actions directly from current observations without additional inference overhead. VLA-$\Delta$ achieves performance competitive with the state of the art on LIBERO and RoboTwin, and outperforms $\pi_{0.5}$ in real-robot experiments. Our code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[ReState: Noise-Level-Aware State Correction for Training-Free Object Removal in Few-Step Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21287)**  `arXiv:2610.21287`  `cs.CV`  
  _Donghwan Lee, Sanghyun Jo, Sihyun Kim, Ziseok Lee, Kyungsu Kim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Training-free object removal commonly redirects self-attention away from the target region, but on few-step distilled diffusion models this often leaves object-shaped residuals. We identify a temporal mismatch: a structured prediction forms inside the mask at the first forward, and the short trajectory offers few later chances to revise it, while attention rerouting acts mainly in later steps. Across four distilled backbones, magnitude-matched single-forward interventions show that background-directed state correction affects the final result more than attention rerouting. We therefore propose ReState, which corrects the masked clean prediction toward background evidence retrieved from the input using a noise-level-aware weight that vanishes at the final forward. ReState retains object-excluding attention as a complementary constraint and adds no denoiser evaluations. Across two paired benchmarks and two few-step backbones, ReState achieves the best masked PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS among training-free methods, improving PSNR by 3.17 dB on average over the strongest baseline. On OBER-Test, it also outperforms the 50-step attention-forcing reference on all three metrics at 6.7x lower latency. The same configuration transfers to two additional distilled backbones without retuning. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[FocusOPD: Structure-Guided On-Policy Distillation for Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21250)**  `arXiv:2610.21250`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xinrui Lin, Shumin Wang, Suqi Song, Fan Li, Yanyong Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-teacher on-policy distillation enables rectified-flow models with diffusion-transformer architectures to combine task-specific capabilities within a single student. However, matching model outputs does not guarantee that their internal representations are aligned, and it remains unclear where and when additional feature supervision is most useful. We analyze the teacher-student feature mismatch across Transformer blocks and denoising timesteps, and find that the mismatch is concentrated in deeper Transformer blocks during early denoising and persists after OPD distillation, indicating ineffective distillation. Motivated by this observation, we introduce FocusOPD, which augments the base OPD objective with token-wise directional feature alignment restricted to deep layers and early denoising steps. Across two rectified-flow backbones and multiple OPD objectives, FocusOPD improves capability transfer in compositional generation, text rendering, and preference-based evaluation. On the primary benchmark, it improves over DiffusionOPD from 0.953 to 0.967 on GenEval, from 23.797 to 23.944 on PickScore, and from 6.052 to 6.199 on LAION-Aesthetic. Ablations show that selective deep and early supervision is more effective and efficient than alignment over the full network and trajectory.
  </details>

- **[SpaceCache: Intermediate Feature Reuse for Efficient Semantic Segmentation on Point Cloud Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21207)**  `arXiv:2610.21207`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dongheon Yang, Beomseok Kang, Jae-Joon Kim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Point-cloud streams are central to autonomous driving and robotics, yet existing segmentation networks process every frame from scratch, although consecutive frames largely overlap. We observe that the intermediate features of point-cloud transformers remain semantically consistent across frames for the same spatial region, especially in deep stages. Building on this, we present SpaceCache, a training-free feature cache that reuses these features across frames. Tokens of previously observed regions skip a sequence of transformer blocks and take their cached features, only the remaining tokens are computed, and the full token layout is restored for dense prediction. Lightweight cache management keeps reused features reliable by limiting each entry's age and evicting low-confidence entries. Unlike token merging, SpaceCache selects tokens at negligible cost and runs its cache operations once per encoder stage. On both outdoor and indoor benchmarks, such as nuScenes, Waymo, and ScanNet, with two transformer backbones of Utonia and LitePT-B, SpaceCache achieves up to 1.65x speedup with less than 1-point mIoU drop and consistently outperforms token-merging methods.
  </details>

- **[ControlFlow3D: Distilling Multi-View Knowledge into Latent Flow Matching for Point Cloud Upsampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21190)**  `arXiv:2610.21190`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuang Liu, Zhi Zuo, Zhengkai Zhao, Lirui Zhang, Pan Gao, Hao Feng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Point cloud upsampling is essential for 3D applications but remains challenging due to sparse, under-constrained observations. Multi-view images can resolve geometric ambiguity, but existing multi-modal methods do not fully exploit visual cues. We propose ControlFlow3D, a framework that distills knowledge from pretrained foundation models into a lightweight generation network during training, enabling strong performance without multi-view images available at test time. Specifically, our method performs conditional flow matching in a structured latent space defined by a frozen 3D encoder. Multi-view image tokens and 3D latent tokens are jointly clustered into shared prototypes via unsupervised soft KMeans; the resulting prototypes guide the flow trajectory through velocity control and are aligned by a consistency loss that progressively transfers image knowledge into the base network. A Mamba-based geometry decoder then maps latent features to dense coordinates with linear complexity. We also construct ShapeNetPU, a 35K-object multi-modal benchmark that is 31 times larger than prior datasets. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of our method and its strong generalization capability.
  </details>

- **[VisualTrust: Video Reasoning under On-Screen Claim Framing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21159)**  `arXiv:2610.21159`  `cs.CV`  
  _Huachao Zhu, Junjielong Xu, Sicheng Li, Liang Yang, Mingtao Chen, Zhichao Hu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  On-screen text can describe what a video shows, or it can frame what the video is supposed to mean. Current benchmarks treat both alike, testing whether a model can read the text rather than whether it should trust it. We study claim deference, multimodal large language models' tendency to let an on-screen claim override conflicting visual evidence. We introduce VisualTrust, a counterfactual benchmark with 5,063 cases across 14 claim-fact classes. Each case pairs a question with three versions of the same video: one with no claim, one with an accurate claim, and one with a misleading claim that alters a single grounded fact. The option the misleading claim supports is the trap answer. Across seven open-weight and four proprietary models, mean accuracy falls from 90.1% without a claim to 51.9% with a misleading claim. Among initially correct responses, 42.8% flip to the trap answer and only 0.6% become other errors. Strong no-claim accuracy does not ensure resistance. We also introduce DEAR (Decoupled Evidence Arbitration and Reading), a training-free agent for video question answering that separates evidence arbitration from reading. An independent check reports conflicting evidence, a selector weighs it without a fixed modality priority and blinds what it withholds, and the reader answers from the resulting video in a fresh context. On 500 videos across three proprietary models, DEAR raises accuracy under misleading claims from 47.2--64.0% to 81.8--85.2% and reduces trap-answer selection from 34.4--50.8% to 11.6--15.0%, while preserving no-claim accuracy.
  </details>

- **[Mind the Tail: Data Selection Through Sparse Concepts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21007)**  `arXiv:2610.21007`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ziheng Zhang, Neha Hulkund, Colin Lee, Zheda Mai, Hui Shen, Ying Ding, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Data selection reallocates training support across visual concepts, including objects, attributes, color, texture, and backgrounds. Different allocation strategies may produce varied downstream model behavior, especially for tail concepts supported by only a few samples. Discarding them can hurt test-time robustness, while overemphasizing samples lying on category boundaries may introduce unstable training signals. This work explicitly investigates this relationship across fine-grained, spurious-correlation, and general image classification, as well as multimodal pretraining. Specifically, we use sparse autoencoders (SAEs) to extract concept activations from individual images. During selection, we accumulate the dataset-level concept support for each added sample. Applying diminishing returns to this distribution down-weights well-represented concepts and shifts the focus to the distribution tail. We then incorporate local support to find samples that reliably strengthen under-supported concepts. Empirical results show consistent improvements over strong data selection baselines across all benchmarks. We also reveal the connection between concepts our method enhances and the induced behavioral changes, including improved robustness against out-of-distribution and spurious contexts. This work establishes concept support, beyond individual sample importance, as a useful principle for data-efficient and robust learning.
  </details>

- **[DeSpatial: The devil of Agentic Spatial Reasoning Lies in 3D Itself](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20990)**  `arXiv:2610.20990`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yifei Liu, Xiaolong Zhou, Ziyang Gong, Yifan Zhou, Yuanyuan Gao, Yuning Gong, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spatial agents have access to reconstruction and segmentation tools, yet inconsistent predictions and fragmented object identities can prevent their outputs from becoming reliable 3D scene information. A simple oracle experiment exposes this perception bottleneck: replacing tool-predicted perception with ground-truth 3D bounding boxes raises an agent to nearly full score across five object-centric VSI-Bench tasks, while retaining the same reasoning tools. Motivated by this finding, we believe the devil of spatial reasoning lies in 3D itself, and introduce DeSpatial(Devil of Spatial), which explicitly decomposes spatial understanding into perception and reasoning. DeSpatial has a shared 3D perception tool to reconcile independent perception outputs into a unified, metric, gravity-aligned 3D scene. This perception tool has two core components: a) a joint segmentation--depth co-refinement module that consolidates masks of the same object and removes projection artifacts caused by inconsistent segmentation and depth within a single frame; b) a cross-view instance fusion module that consolidate voxel-overlapping instances but not allowed to merge same-frame instances. This fusion module additionally fuses spatially disjoint fragments caused by insufficient multi-view observations, by checking their 3D structure and source mask appearance. After receiving the complete and unified 3D scene information, the agent chooses reasoning or computational tools to solve the spatial problem. DeSpatial achieves leading performance on VSI-Bench, MindCube-Tiny, and ViewSpatial, with additional long-video results on VSI-SUPER, revealing the bottleneck of spatial understanding lies in a precise 3D perception itself.
  </details>

- **[Blame Errant Words, Not the Canvas: Defect-Aware Spatial Credit Assignment for Visual Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20794)**  `arXiv:2610.20794`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sujie Hu, Huaisong Zhang, Pujing Yang, Qian Wang, Xiaomin Li, Xinhang Leng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent reinforcement learning approaches have improved visual text generation, yet generated images still suffer from incorrect wording and malformed glyphs. We identify two coupled limitations: recognition-based rewards often overlook visible glyph defects, while whole-image scalar feedback obscures the uneven quality of individual text regions. These limitations motivate our central principle: $\textit{Blame errant words, not the canvas}$, to effectively preserve local quality distinctions. First, we introduce $\mathbf{TextDART}$, a defect-aware region-targeted reward model that localizes individual text regions and independently evaluates their semantic correctness and glyph quality, providing fine-grained, region-precise feedback. Building on TextDART, we propose $\mathbf{SCANNER}$, which turns these regional judgments into spatially differentiated policy feedback. It normalizes rewards for each text target across sampled images and casts the resulting regional advantages onto the corresponding latent tokens. Extensive experiments on existing benchmarks and our newly established $\textbf{PolyText-Bench}$ demonstrate that our approach achieves state-of-the-art performance in text accuracy and glyph fidelity. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Orbia: Do Generated Videos Form a Consistent 3D World?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20693)**  `arXiv:2610.20693`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dongyue Lu, Guian Fang, Hur Lim, Yonggang Wu, Yanrong Wang, Hongshan Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video generators serve as interactive worlds for exploration and simulation, yet realistic and visually appealing frames do not necessarily depict a consistent 3D world. Existing benchmarks assess selected geometric properties or use vision-language models to judge memory and consistency, leaving systematic 3D evaluation underexplored. We introduce Orbia, the first benchmark dedicated to a systematic and comprehensive evaluation of 3D consistency in interactive world models. Through controlled exploration and revisit, we organize the evaluation into three dimensions: input preservation, generated-content persistence, and 3D self-consistency. For input preservation, registered RGB-D references and annotated anchors support appearance, geometry, and spatial stability measurements. For generated-content persistence, we compare revisits to regions absent from the input image with their first generated observations to test whether models preserve what they create. For 3D self-consistency, cross-view depth and feature correspondence, regional scale agreement, and held-out Gaussian rendering test whether generated views describe the same 3D scene. Minute-long exploration and revisits further test long-term consistency. Our construction and annotation pipeline brings together public datasets, self-captured scenes, and simulation, yielding 720 instances across indoor and outdoor environments and six exploration-and-revisit protocols. Evaluation spans 27 text-, action-, and camera-conditioned models through native-interface adapters. Our Orbia benchmark provides a systematic basis for diagnosing 3D inconsistency and developing more persistent video world models.
  </details>

- **[A Diagnostic Study of World-Space Human Recovery Under High Camera Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19729)**  `arXiv:2610.19729`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hao-Jen Chien, Wei-Lun Chao, Yu-Lun Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Monocular world-space human recovery methods excel on current benchmarks, but these rarely include fast camera motion. We introduce the K-Pop stress test, broadcast dance videos that combine fast, large camera motion with dense multi-person choreography. Leading methods produce plausible per-frame meshes on these videos, yet their world-space trajectories drift far from the performers' stage positions. Through a systematic diagnosis, we show that the camera and human estimates each remain self-consistent, and that the drift appears when they are combined at mismatched scales. Each is recovered at its own scale, so a mismatch between the two can turn camera motion into human drift that grows with camera displacement and becomes severe in broadcast dance videos. We derive a closed-form correction that estimates this mismatch and applies one scale to all performers, removing the drift while keeping their relative positions in one shared world frame. It requires no retraining and works with existing backends. Combined with physical constraints, the correction yields stable world-space reconstruction for choreography modeling, scene editing, data generation, and virtual reshooting. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[DuplexBridge: Coordinating Understanding and Generation for Unified Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18819)**  `arXiv:2610.18819`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yidi Liu, Jie Xiao, Chaojie Mao, Jingfeng Zhang, Xueyang Fu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Complex image editing requires faithful task interpretation and accurate execution, but a failed edit does not reveal which responsibility failed. We present DuplexBridge, which uses a unified model's own understanding capability to enhance prompts for its own generation capability, without an external understanding module. A source-grounded bridge specifies targets, final-state changes, and preservation constraints. Understanding is supervised on fidelity to the original request without observing the generated image; generation is supervised on execution of the condition it actually receives. Reciprocal trust checks selectively admit global task feedback to each role while retaining independently valid local supervision. We introduce CE-Bench, a 1,200-task benchmark measuring joint satisfaction of task-derived change and preservation requirements. Across three model configurations, the full system improves complete compliance over direct editors by 21.75-25.75 percentage points, with gains also observed on KRIS-Bench, RISEBench, ImgEdit, and GEdit-Bench. From a common supervised Wan-Image initialization, gated joint training reaches 77.08% complete compliance, compared with 70.92% without gates and 75.00% when generation-local supervision instead checks the original task with both gates retained. These results support distinct local responsibilities and selective shared feedback for joint understanding-generation adaptation.
  </details>

- **[RSIgen: Reciprocal Self-Improvement for Agentic Visual Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18688)**  `arXiv:2610.18688`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yidi Liu, Haozhe Wang, Jie Xiao, Kai Zhu, Xueyang Fu, Zheng-Jun Zha_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Agentic visual generation produces experience that can improve the native understanding (agent) and generation roles of a unified multimodal backbone, but task success alone does not determine which role should learn. Generator updates can also make the agent's action supervision stale. We introduce RSIgen, a task-centric reciprocal self-improvement framework linking learning allocation, supervision refresh, and recursive curriculum construction. Dynamic Responsibility Routing (DRR) uses matched interventions on evidence, inputs, and supported actions to select learning recipients and construct compatible training targets. After generator learning, RSIgen re-evaluates the relative utility of tested actions with branch inputs held fixed to refresh agent supervision; the retained roles then construct the next curriculum. Across BAGEL, Qwen-Image, and Wan-Image-7B, RSIgen improves AgentGen-Bench Full Overall9 by 3.2--3.3 points over a three-cycle SearchGen adaptation with action costs. Wan controls support allocation gains at matched role-level training exposure, refresh gains at a matched adaptation budget, and benefits from updated curriculum sources while recipients continue learning. In blind evaluation on 200 real-user requests, pooled tie-adjusted preference scores against the same baseline reach 70.0% for task completion and 64.9% for visual quality.
  </details>

- **[TwinPrune: Density-Aware Two-Phase Token Pruning for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17530)**  `arXiv:2610.17530`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kai Liu, Anqi Li, Junxian Li, Zhixin Wang, Zhikai Chen, Renjing Pei, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We propose a unified spatial-density signal to score token importance for both inference phases: bipartite merge for prefill and KV eviction for decode, yielding measurable gains in each stage. To isolate the two phases, we split benchmarks by scoring logic. Prefill probes (MMBench, POPE, ScienceQA) evaluate only the first decoded token; their accuracy reflects prefill logits argmax before any decode KV eviction. Full-pipeline probes (TextVQA, DocVQA, MM-Vet) assess the complete generated sequence and capture both phases. We identify a critical evaluation pitfall: decode pruning claims validated solely on prefill probes measure prefill quality, not decode performance. Experiments show discarding all visual KV during decode barely affects prefill-probe accuracy but degrades TextVQA and MM-Vet drastically; this insensitivity stems from the scoring rule, not compression intensity. Evaluated on seven model variants (InternVL-3.5, Qwen3-VL, LLaVA-1.5) and three full-pipeline benchmarks, our method achieves a three-way Pareto frontier across compute, memory and accuracy. Density-based prefill compression maintains strong accuracy, scales linearly with visual token count, and delivers 100x faster token selection at high resolution. For decode, density top-k KV eviction consistently outperforms strong baselines H2O, SnapKV and StreamingLLM on TextVQA and MM-Vet across three retention ratios. At 0.125 keep ratio on TextVQA, it surpasses H2O by 9.6 points; InternVL-3.5 models (2B to 38B) maintain a 7 to 10 point advantage. Compression scales favorably with model size: every measured 8B to 38B model transition reduces accuracy loss instead of worsening it. We suggest MM-Vet or DocVQA as mandatory full-pipeline validation for any decode-phase claims. Our code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Image Tokenizers Should Be Trained Between Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16759)**  `arXiv:2610.16759`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ole Beißwenger, Jan-Niklas Dihlmann, Hendrik Lensch_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Latent diffusion models generate images as codes of an image tokenizer, whose decoder turns them into the pixel image. Recent tokenizers improve how well these codes reconstruct an image and how easily a generator learns them. Yet a generator does not reproduce the codes of training images, and when a generated code lands outside what the decoder expects, the decoded image contains imperfections. Current methods make the decoder more robust to generated codes by adding noise during training. However, generated codes lie between the training codes and resemble their mixture more than one of them plus noise. To improve generated images, decoders should therefore be trained on codes in the space between training samples. We propose InterpTok, which fine-tunes MAETok to decode mixtures of image codes into natural images. Since no target image exists between two training samples, a discriminator judges whether each decoded mixture looks as natural as its two sources. A mixing ratio loss requires the decoded mixture to keep the mixing ratio of the two codes. Together, they prevent collapse to a trivial solution and yield a sharp and natural composition of both images. InterpTok keeps MAETok's reconstruction quality and improves interpolation quality more than threefold, the best of all tokenizers in a recent benchmark. Beyond interpolation, InterpTok improves the generation FID of a matched diffusion transformer from 11.61 to 8.48 without guidance, and even reaches 8.11 at an eighth of the sampling steps, where the generator on MAETok's codes worsens. The generator trained on our tokenizer also reaches the final result of the one trained on MAETok at half the training steps. Our findings show that the space between image codes can and should be explicitly supervised rather than ignored.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Entropy: Self-Diagnostic Multi-Role Token Optimization for Video Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03400)**  `arXiv:2610.03400`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yudong Han, Yong Wang, Zaiquan Yang, Liang Lin, Chongyang Tao, Xiangxiang Chu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards has substantially advanced multimodal reasoning, yet it remains fundamentally limited by ambiguous token-level credit assignment. While high-entropy token heuristics encourage possibility exploration, naively extending them to video reasoning tends to induce lengthy reasoning, as the model becomes overly reliant on high-entropy visual activations. Alternative approaches that rely on counterfactual-based visual token localization for credit assignment also tend to over-prioritize visual exploration at the expense of decisive reasoning cues for answer derivation, thereby exacerbating the interference from spurious visual nuances. Moreover, these methods employ static counterfactual strategies that fail to co-evolve with the policy during training. In this paper, we introduce DyCPO, a co-evolutionary framework that jointly optimizes reliable token selection and adaptive counterfactual intervention. It constructs a multi-role dependence metric to balance visual exploration and answer-relevance mining in token-wise contrastive learning, while suppressing exploration-only filler tokens and spurious visual noise. Rather than relying on static counterfactual priors, DyCPO dynamically derives counterfactual signals from the model's own successful and failed rollouts, enabling self-diagnostic analysis and co-evolution of the optimization objective with the policy. Extensive experiments on complex video reasoning and general video understanding benchmarks demonstrate consistent performance improvements, establishing DyCPO as a robust token-level credit assignment paradigm for multimodal reinforcement learning.
  </details>

- **[TRAC: Trajectory-aware Reuse and Adaptive Correction for Efficient Autoregressive Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02779)**  `arXiv:2610.02779`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiaxing Song, Weiqi Yan, You Huang, Mingte Qiu, Huazhong Liu, Xiaofeng Zhu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autoregressive (AR) video generation enables scalable long-video synthesis by sequentially generating fixed-length chunks, but incurs substantial computational cost from repeated denoising and attention over growing historical contexts. Recent cache reuse methods exploit temporal redundancy to reduce this cost, yet are mainly designed for single-trajectory generation, making them less suitable for sequentially coupled AR trajectories where approximation errors can accumulate and propagate across chunks. To address this challenge, we propose TRAC, a Trajectory-aware Reuse and Adaptive Correction framework for efficient and robust AR video generation. TRAC integrates three complementary components: (1) Robust Cumulative Scheduling (RCS), which selects cache reuse schedules by accounting for cumulative rollout errors and their variation across chunks and prompts; (2) Autoregressive Trajectory-aware Guidance Scheduling (ATGS), which coordinates classifier-free guidance refreshes along the global AR trajectory; and (3) Spectral Structure Correction (SSC), which adaptively restores low-frequency structural information to mitigate long-term error accumulation. By combining these innovations, TRAC substantially improves the efficiency-quality trade-off of AR video generation. For example, on VBench with SkyReels-V2, it achieves a 6.03x speedup over the vanilla model with only ~0.6% VBench score degradation, while consistently outperforming existing AR acceleration methods in both latency and generation quality. The code is publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[LVMT: Video Mask Transformer for Long-term Video Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34895)**  `arXiv:2609.34895`  `cs.CV`  
  _Narges Norouzi, Niccolò Cavagnero, Idil Esen Zulfikar, Bastian Leibe, Gijs Dubbelman, Daan de Geus_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing online video segmentation methods struggle to track objects in long, complex videos with long-term occlusions. We hypothesize that this limitation is caused by (i) the inability of their temporal propagation mechanism to adaptively select the object information that is propagated across time, and (ii) their inability to be trained on long videos due to memory requirements and vanishing gradients. To address the first limitation, we propose to use a lightweight GRU-based temporal propagation module that can learn to select which information it keeps in memory and propagates across time. Second, to allow training on long videos, we introduce Truncated Query Propagation (TQP), a training strategy in which the model processes a video in chunks of frames, where information about tracked objects is propagated between chunks but backpropagation is only conducted in individual chunks, enabling longer temporal supervision without out-of-memory issues, inference overhead, or vanishing gradients. The resulting model is called the Long-term Video Mask Transformer (LVMT). Extensive experiments on six benchmarks show that LVMT sets a new state of the art across a range of video segmentation tasks, while retaining the speed of the highly efficient model it is based on, making it 10X faster than the prior state of the art. Code:this https URL
  </details>

- **[MiCo: Mutual Information Coverage Optimization through Semantic Erasure Modeling for Efficient MLLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34330)**  `arXiv:2609.34330`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tinghao Wang, Yichen Guo, Qizhe Zhang, Yuan Zhang, Weimin Ouyang, Rui Huang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated impressive performance in multimodal understanding, but processing large numbers of visual tokens results in high computational costs. While many methods have been proposed to reduce the number of visual tokens, most of them rely on heuristics and are prone to discarding substantial visual information during pruning, leading to degradation in model performance. In this work, by using a semantic erasure model, we derive a general mutual information coverage objective from task log-loss and propose MiCo, a training-free two-stage pruning method. MiCo first uses visual signals to select a representative candidate pool before visual tokens enter the language model, then performs task-aware subset selection within it. At each stage, suitable observable proxies instantiate the derived objective as a monotone submodular coverage function, which MiCo greedily optimizes under the token budget. MiCo is evaluated on diverse MLLMs ranging from 7B to 13B parameters across a broad range of image and video benchmarks spanning general visual reasoning, fine-grained OCR and grounding, hallucination detection, and long-video understanding. MiCo consistently achieves the best performance across nearly all evaluated models under all pruning ratios. On LLaVA-NEXT-13B, MiCo uses only 5.6% visual tokens, retains 97.5% of baseline performance, and achieves a 3.8-fold inference speedup. Our experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of MiCo and our mutual information coverage objective for visual token pruning. Our code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[ME-VLM: A Unified VLM for Embodied Cognition and Agent Coordination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24526)**  `arXiv:2609.24526`  `cs.CV`  
  _Foundation Model, Li Auto Inc_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Physical AI requires models to ground visual and linguistic understanding in real-world environments while accounting for environmental constraints and execution feedback. We introduce MachEmbodied-VLM (ME-VLM), a unified vision-language model with two variants, 4B and 35B-A3B, that brings together embodied cognition and multimodal agent capabilities. Our work emphasizes physical perception and spatiotemporal reasoning, together with planning, interaction, and outcome assessment in both digital and physical environments. We construct training data spanning embodied and multimodal agent tasks, including execution observations and feedback to support outcome assessment and decision refinement. The training pipeline comprises embodied capability injection, separate reinforcement learning of embodied and multimodal-agent experts, and multi-teacher on-policy distillation that consolidates their complementary capabilities into a single model. Experiments show competitive performance on both embodied and agent benchmarks, as well as on autonomous-driving and embodied-navigation tasks.
